the rapporteur. - (da) mr president, it is only natural that wealthy western europe should act as a magnet for all those seeking protection and all those fleeing persecution. we must of course contribute to the efforts required by an increasing influx of refugees in proportions and volumes commensurate with our level of prosperity and our historical role in the world. but it's not always easy to bring people from different cultures together. the commission has already taken a number of measures to help the refugees, and this is why these people must be put on the back foot and we must also help those who have fled their country to start a new life elsewhere. member states are tackling these problems in very different ways, and this is quite normal given the existence of different cultures and traditions. but all refugees have the same basic needs, and some groups need professional help and support to be able to resume a human existence. in addition, they need advice and guidance in relation to the authorities of the new country as well as in relation to the asylum application itself. all refugees dream of going home. for some, this dream will come true, but for others, the time spent in exile will be so long that a return seems unrealistic. they have children and friends, they lead a new life; their children take root in the host country and these roots become stronger than those of their parents in their native country. this is why some may ultimately decide to apply for citizenship of their new country, but several studies have shown that it is essential, even for people who are fulfilling their dream of returning to their home country, to give meaning to their period of exile, to allow them in the meantime to develop as human beings, to allow them to use their qualifications in order to avoid being stuck or handicapped by a period of inactivity, passivity, uncertainty or by an inability to recycle themselves in order to keep up with developments in their field of activity. the terms integration and repatriation are therefore not contradictory, and integration is not just for people who want to change their country permanently. integration is also an important help for those who return home. it may even be a condition for successful repatriation. so i cannot understand why some people can make a distinction between refugees and displaced persons. the tasks which europe has to perform are not distributed equally among all the member states; it depends on the size of the state and its prosperity. the european refugee fund represents a very modest first attempt at allocating resources in relation to the tasks incumbent on each member state. it is not, of course, the ratio of the number of refugees to the number of citizens of origin; it is the actual tasks to be performed. it is not the relative number that is in question, but the actual number. i think that a number of the amendments tabled by the epp and eldr groups are based on a misunderstanding of this. i hope this discussion will clarify the intentions a little. i am of course sorry that, in view of the scale of the task and the unequal distribution of resources between the member states, the fund has been allocated so little. the budget is €26 million for current affairs, fixed expenditure, to be distributed among the 15 member states. this leaves little room for the member states, which have indeed assumed a great deal of responsibility. in germany or italy, this must be what denmark calls a "snowball in hell", that is, something very inadequate. on the other hand, there is some uncertainty about the financial prospects of the fund. i look forward to hearing whether the commissioner has any good news to tell us and whether the commission has considered what the future prospects of the fund would be. i hope it's not just wind for the next few years. the european refugee fund is a start. and if we start well and if we manage to follow its evolution, we will obviously have to increase its resources so that it lives up to its name - the european refugee fund.
- (da) mr president, mrs pernille frahm and her party are waging a fierce battle in denmark against what they call the 'more union' principle. they campaign against denmark's participation in the common currency - a subject which will be the subject of a referendum in september - and in the five referendums that have already been held in denmark, they have voted against the union on four occasions. that is why i am delighted to read the report by mrs pernille frahm, because she not only recognizes but also calls for 'more union' in the form of mutual aid in the face of the burden of refugee flows. it also called on the national authorities to transfer more funds to the european refugee fund. i'm with you on that. if large numbers of albanian refugees arrive in italy or if large numbers of refugees from the baltic states arrive in scandinavia, it is preferable to spread the expenditure across the whole union rather than forcibly dispersing the refugees in the different member states of the union. the refugee fund will help more refugees in areas close to their home countries, where they can maintain ties with their families and their culture of origin and keep the pressure on the tyrants who expelled them. i therefore welcome the eu's decision to unleash its purse strings to meet the needs of these large flows of refugees. we saw the need for this during the kosovo war. imagine the situation if many more kosovars could have received aid in macedonia and albania - the border countries! they would have preferred this to being deported thousands of miles from their homeland. so mrs pernille frahm's party and mine agree on one thing. could we not, mr president, ask mrs pernille frahm to be the rapporteur on monetary cooperation; i am sure we could then agree.
- (da) mr president, let me first of all point out that in the 1993 referendum on the edinburgh agreement the danish people refused to participate in judicial cooperation, so that denmark is not bound by any obligation to let the eu decide which refugees denmark should accept. so you cannot decently accept eu money if you refuse to let the eu decide how refugees are distributed among member countries. i believe that it is up to each country to finance its own refugee policy; i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware of the fact that the commission has not yet adopted a proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks arising from exposure to asbestos. representatives of the high commission for refugees in geneva reported on danish television that only one in ten asylum seekers could legitimately be accepted as a refugee. once most refugees are granted asylum or residence permits, as in denmark, this is tantamount to abandoning legitimate refugees. denmark is the country in the world that provides the highest level of financial assistance to refugees; the european union has a duty to help the refugees, and it is a duty to help the refugees. i agree with mrs frahm on one point, however: the 26 million euros allocated are far too little. in denmark alone, the level of aid reaches eur 2 billion per year. so a fund that small cannot work. we therefore believe that the problems of allocation and prioritisation of action to help refugees should be tackled in a much more rational way.
- (da) mr president, our foodstuffs - and this also applies to fresh products such as the peeled carrots we have been talking about today - are nowadays denatured in every possible way, whether by means of colourings, sweeteners, flavouring substances, preservatives, etc. and always with the same goal: to hide the natural properties of the product. it is not, moreover, a limited number of substances which are in question; we use thousands of additives today, most of which we don't know about, and the health consequences of. we know that many of them are toxic, and yet we allow them to be used. the community's policy on the use of additives has, in my view, become totally irresponsible. that is why i am happy to support mr lannoye's report, which i think is a good piece of work. we must refuse new additives and we must refuse to increase the number of substances authorized and permitted. i think, on the contrary, that the commission should seriously reduce the number of additives authorized. mr lannoye's report, in its general remarks, mentions many substances which should be studied, including the direct addition of antibiotics in food. i would like to point out another problem, and it was mentioned by mr lannoye in his speech; the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the use of nitrates in meat products. i think that these substances are allowed in far too large quantities. a large study carried out by five consumer associations in europe recently showed that many meat products contained very high levels of nitrosamines. nitrosamines are carcinogenic and are derived from nitrates and nitrites. i think that the situation is unacceptable in this area, but i have not heard any reaction from the commission following this study. i believe that we must reduce very substantially the amount of nitrates and nitrites added to meat products and i believe that there should be mandatory controls on the amounts of nitrosamines, which - i repeat - are very carcinogenic, although this substance is not even measured in our food at present. i hope that commissioner byrne, who is now responsible for this area, will look into the question of this addition of large quantities of nitrates and nitrites. this is a serious problem and i hope the commissioner will respond favourably to my request. i think we need to reduce the amount of additives in our food very substantially.
- (da) mr president, let me say, as an introduction, that each report by mr paul lannoye is very competently drafted and i fully agree with all its conclusions. as you know, three conditions must be met for additives to be included on the positive list. there must be a technological need, the product must be useful to the consumer and its safety must have been proven. mr paul lannoye rightly points out that none of these conditions are met for sodium alginate. this substance is not useful to the consumer; on the contrary, it is quite misleading by making the cut carrots appear fresh when they are not. it is also very regrettable that the synergy effects have not been studied. it is, in principle, very regrettable that the commission should be able to extend the list of additives simply because these products are authorized in certain parts of the eu. this rule should be amended so that an additive can only be included in the positive list if there is a clearly demonstrated technological need, which is of course an advantage for consumers. it is paradoxical that we should have to consider this proposal so soon after the publication of the commission's white paper on foodstuffs. the commission insists in its white paper on the need to limit the addition of additives in order - and it is the commission that says this - to avoid adverse health consequences. the commission also states that it wishes to provide consumers with the best possible information to enable them to make their own choices. examination of the present proposal for a directive shows that there is very little coherence between words and deeds in the way the eu approaches the problem of additives. if i remember correctly, the original purpose of drawing up a positive list was also to allow the exclusion of certain additives from that list. the current liberalization of the number of authorised additives is a cause for concern. so let's first narrow down the list rather than expand it. following mr lund's comments on nitrites and nitrates, i should add that i asked the commission at the last part-session whether the new scientific evidence on the toxic effects of these two substances on health had not led the commission to reconsider its position. the answer was unfortunately no and, like mr lund, i would now like the commission to justify its refusal.

- (da) mr president, in denmark there is a concept called selskabstømning (society vacancy). good lawyers and corporate auditors are being tried for their active part in "emptying" corporate assets not satisfied with the laws, we can elect new representatives who will pass new laws. this is the heartbeat of our democracies. the heart of democracy has stopped beating because of the constitution dumpsters. they have taken legislative power from our 15 national parliaments and handed it over to civil servants and ministers, they have transferred that power from the hands of the electors and elected representatives of the people to the executive, from public hearings to closed doors in brussels. the power of the electors has left our constitutions, but it has not been passed on to the european parliament. we, who sit here, are a garage door. the power is in the commission and the council of ministers. the european union is not a parliamentary democracy, and national parliaments are even more remote. that is why several of us in this house have drawn up 19 proposals for a democratic reform of the european union. we call this initiative sos democracy. sos because it's urgent, democracy because it's the crux of the problem. the european union is not a democratic union at the moment. we would like to see democracy in the future. we have several ideas on the legislative aspect. we have the support of many groups, from the communists to the conservatives, with whom we disagree on many things, but we agree that our disagreement must be settled in democratic negotiations on the treaty of nice before it is too late. please join sos democracy, mr president.

in writing. - the danish social democrats in the european parliament voted today against the dimitrakopoulos and leinen report on parliament's proposal for the intergovernmental conference. we believe that by insisting on a broad agenda the european parliament is not serving what should be the main task of the intergovernmental conference, namely to resolve the outstanding institutional problems on the formation and voting rules of the council, the commission, the european parliament, the court of justice and other institutions, which will make enlargement possible. the european parliament should rather raise the important problem of ensuring that important issues that need to be resolved are dealt with in such a way that european citizens are involved and interested in the important issues that are being debated. the european union must be based on a common political will to achieve the higher goals of peace, the environment and well-being in europe, with citizens at its centre. the reading in the committee on institutional affairs, which adopted the preliminary draft report by a very small majority, followed by the fact that the reading in plenary resulted in the submission of more than 200 amendments, shows that this report does not contribute to the formation of a common basis for the intergovernmental conference. apart from the fact that we do not think that the european parliament should propose all these amendments to the treaty, we have chosen to look at each point, as will be shown by our vote. we will therefore specially point out the points on which we believe the conference should concentrate. as regards the composition of the european parliament, we voted for the number of member states to be calculated in a decreasing manner so that the parliament reflects the population of each country, but also the fact that the representation of the different political views of each country is an essential value for the representativeness of the european parliament. as regards the composition of the commission, it is essential that all countries have the right to have a commissioner with the same rights and duties. this must be seen in particular in the light of the fact that the commission must, in our view, continue to enjoy the right of initiative. as regards the voting rules in the council, we believe that a balance must be struck between the respective considerations of the small and the large countries, which must be achieved by requiring a fixed number of votes in the council, which must at the same time represent a simple majority of the total population of the union. we are open to any discussion of increased use of qualified majority voting, but we refuse to accept that this should become the rule. a move to qualified majority voting must in any case be analysed and debated in depth, because a balance must be struck between what is fundamental for each country and the fact that cooperation must work. it is particularly important that this subject is constantly discussed with european citizens. all member countries must show political will to make cooperation work. it is on this will and desire to see a common solution that the eu will have to be based in the future. we are therefore not worried that the union will be sclerosed after enlargement if the treaty is not thoroughly revised now. in parallel with the intergovernmental conference, the member states discuss a common defence and security policy. these discussions are by no means over, and we do not think that the european parliament should propose amendments to the treaty until the outcome of these debates is known. we still do not believe that the eu should become a real defence alliance. in this area, the union should focus on conflict prevention and civil crisis management. the creation of a charter of fundamental rights is also being discussed on the sidelines of the intergovernmental conference. we want to stress once again the wish that this charter should be seen as a political document and not be brought to the table of the intergovernmental conference with a view to its inclusion in the treaty.

- (da) mr president, for years i have been advocating the modification of the milk distribution system in schools. i think this system is completely unconsidered. from a food standpoint, it is totally absurd, and impossible to manage. at the moment, we are subsidizing whole milk, half-skimmed milk and no skimmed milk at all. the purpose of such regulations is to reduce surpluses, and while there is more fat in whole milk, it is this type of milk that is most subsidized. we should instead be giving a grant per student. i would have preferred to see prices reduced even more, in order to get rid of the bureaucratic milk quotas. in the long term we will have to change the agricultural policy of the european union so that we can eliminate artificially high prices and surplus stocks. but the school milk scheme is one of the best. it's not just for the peasants. the diet is also beneficial to students and their parents, especially if it is modified to subsidize low-fat foods. children don't need a lot of fat. in addition, the student grant scheme is much easier to administer. there is a large administration that looks after the current regime. i've had a lot of complaints from schools that couldn't handle the management of the plan. it's a science to calculate the percentage of fat in milk and cheese. the schools often had to pay back large sums. not because they were cheating, but because they simply couldn't manage the distribution system. that is why i am in favour of voting on the amendments tabled by my friend and colleague, mr niels busk. the system he proposes is the only one that is appropriate from the administrative and food point of view.
- (da) mr president, i should like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mr maat, for his report, which i fully support. i also thank you for the excellent cooperation you have always had with the committee on agriculture. i also thank my colleague and friend freddy blak of the socialist group for his support for my amendments. the milk distribution scheme in schools is a health and nutrition issue. children and young people need to develop healthy eating habits, and milk with its high protein and calcium content is an important component of this. on behalf of the liberal group, mr pesälä and i tabled three draft amendments which are intended not only to make the milk distribution system much easier to administer but also to make the system more flexible as regards subsidized products. there are several traditions as to the types of milk products consumed, and the system is simplified in that the subsidy is distributed on the basis of a quarter of a litre of milk per pupil per school day. for products other than whole milk, the subsidy must be calculated on the basis of a low-fat dry matter or a maximum milk fat content of 50%. the system therefore takes account of the differences in milk traditions in the various member states.

- (da) mr president, the support scheme for flax and hemp has become a real bounty hunt. this regulation has been totally flouted and it is becoming more and more like organized crime. that's why it has to end. for many years, support for hemp has been much higher than for other crops. the hemp support per hectare is twice as high as the cereals support per hectare. this means that despite the fact that there is almost no legal use of hemp, the area used for hemp cultivation has increased fourfold in the past year. the desire to maintain a high level of support for hemp is the result of a very strange collaboration between people who want hashish to be legalised on the one hand and those who speculate on community aid measures on the other. it is, in fact, impossible to distinguish between hemp free of cannabinol and hemp used for hemp production. the commission proposes to reduce support for hemp in three stages in order to reach the level of support for cereals. i would have preferred that the support measures for hemp were immediately reduced to the level of those for cereals, but i will nevertheless support the commission's proposal. this will give farmers time to adapt. i cannot, however, accept the recommendation made by the committee on agriculture. a further extension of the transitional regime is not compatible with the desire to establish a modern, progressive and enlargement-oriented agricultural policy. in other words, it would be a bad idea and i would vote against it. as regards flax, the commission also proposes a reduction in the opportunities for premium hunting and the committee on agriculture again proposes to improve the regulation. i think, on the contrary, that the aid should be reduced. it is up to the market and consumers to decide what is to be produced and not to a community support scheme of gigantic proportions.

- (da) mr president, mr bösch has drawn up a good report on fraud protection. we agree with him on the description of the problems, but i do not think we will succeed in fighting crime and fraudulent enrichment by establishing a common prosecutor's office, establishing common criminal provisions and hiring more european controllers. the most powerful tool we have to do the job is, in my view, transparency - total transparency in the budgetary procedure, total transparency in the names of the people who have decided to grant the appropriations, total transparency in the name of the beneficiary, full access to all the accompanying documents for the ombudsman, the controllers and the committee on budgetary control of the european parliament and the national parliaments. it is by knowing at all times who has received what that journalists and a critical public opinion will uncover cases of fraud; the commission has already proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the proposal. i therefore ask the commissioners to ensure that from now on the assembly, the court of auditors and the ombudsman can examine any annexed document. what are they hiding from us ? are there things the ombudsman can't see? when will we have a record of all the committees and working parties and of the participants in their meetings? will our rapporteurs be able to check every single payment from the funds? obscurity is the best friend of fraudsters. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the commission's proposals are implemented, and i am sure that the commission will be able to do so.

in writing. - (da) support for agriculture is a heavy burden for the european union. the need for a radical revision of support schemes is particularly acute as a result of the enlargement to include the countries of eastern europe. i am pleased to see that the first step towards such a review includes a long-term plan to reduce, to some extent, support for agriculture. it is not right, however, that we should not dare to discuss frankly and openly how our agricultural sector could, in the long run, continue to operate under different conditions and with a support system which is totally different from the present system. we must reduce aid to the minimum and we must discuss it now. secondly, the reduction of agricultural support measures must be gradual and must be carried out at a pace which does not create the risk that national compensatory schemes will replace the lost community aid. it is also not normal for the commission to withdraw its support for agriculture in order to use the available resources for other purposes. a reduction in support measures cannot be motivated by irrelevant objectives.
commission - (es) the introduction of the euro will increase economic integration and reinforce the positive effects of the single market, mainly through the elimination of exchange rate risks. but also thanks to other factors: greater price transparency, improved competition and increased trade and investment both across borders and between other countries or in the rest of the area of countries concerned by monetary union. at the same time, the common monetary policy, in so far as interest rates will be the same in all member states, will have an impact from the demand side, but will also mean a greater integration of financial markets. this should also be beneficial for savers and investors. for all this to have the greatest positive final impact, it is essential to pursue a policy of fiscal consolidation that does not create any strain on monetary policy, and, in parallel, a policy of determined structural reforms and a competition policy that allows the greatest benefits to be obtained from the process. for economic and monetary union to be a success, it is not necessary to have a large budget to allow for cyclical transfers in difficult times. i believe that the treaty, supplemented by the stability and growth pact, provides the necessary instruments for implementing an economic policy consistent with the unified monetary policy. the fundamental framework we have introduced is a framework for mutual surveillance of national fiscal policies and coordination of economic policies through processes of improving our structural situations and coordinating or creating a positive macroeconomic environment that promotes growth and employment, while reducing the risks of asymmetric shocks induced by economic policies. the budgetary consolidation itself, the reduction - of course - of the public deficit, gives member states greater leeway in case of difficulty. it is true that greater mobility in the workplace can also help to cope with certain problems that may arise in the event of asymmetric shocks. both the increase in labour supply and the improvement in productivity would improve the conditions for job creation and reduce unemployment. this is why we attach particular importance to good labour mobility. the commission has therefore emphasized this aspect of labour mobility in its various strategies, while being aware that it is a difficult issue. difficult between member states but also between one member state and another, not only because of training, historical and cultural problems but also because of elements linked to these types of situations. in conclusion, i would say that, of course, these emerging difficulties - which may undoubtedly arise in the future - will certainly be much less important than those put forward theoretically by the specialists in monetary union, since - and this is the experience we have had up to now - we are seeing a convergence of the economic policy situations of the countries and we have created a margin, in budgetary terms, allowing us to face the difficulties with fewer problems than in the past.
- (da) i thank you for your reply, which contains, on the whole, most of the information i expected. i would like to continue with your answer and ask you what you mean when you say that the euro project will only be successful if we have a large budget to solve the problems of changing circumstances. what do you mean, a big budget? do you want to talk about a large community budget? i would also like to know whether it is possible to pursue a policy based on recommendations and requests to the euro-area countries. denmark, for example, has repeatedly ignored the commissioner's recommendations. will this still be accepted in the long term or will a different working model be used in the context of the implementation of the stability and growth pact and in order to ensure stability around emu than the recommendations and requests?
commission - (de) mr president, following the decision of the luxembourg european council in december 1997, the applicant countries were invited to adopt the entire acquis communautaire, i.e. all the legal acts of the european union, without any exception, in the field of legal approximation. to this day, no one has counted them, so i can't say how many. we are talking about some 20 000 legal acts which would be the subject of this case.
- (da) mr president, i really see no reason to thank the commission. it is, in my view, totally inconceivable that an institution should not be able to draw up basic statistics on existing rules, should not be able to calculate the amounts sent to the applicant countries, should not be able to carry out a general numbering so that ordinary citizens can follow what is happening and should not be able to provide the whole acquis on cd-rom to all those who are interested. it's very basic; i've been taking care of him for many years. a company with university graduates is totally unable to find the volume of the acquis and mr vanhengen is apparently unable to give the whole of the acquis to a member who would like to examine it and allocate tasks among different staff in order to know what to do in a particular case. i really have no reason to thank you.
- (da) mr president, this report is contradictory. point 14 supports the requirement in the stability pact that price stability must always take precedence over employment, while other points express the wish to improve employment and increase democratic influence on economic policy. but inflation must always be between zero and two percent. we support this idea, although inflation in germany and denmark reached this level only in the six years after the war. it is obvious that this development will always be the best for all countries and all times. we no longer need elected representatives of the people to formulate the right policy mix. we have a central bank, which sets interest rates based on the level of inflation. the euro may thus fall and interest rates will be raised; growth may decline and the unemployment rate will rise again. it is worse than the sacred cows in india, but the report does not protest against this system. point g reveals a new sacred cow: the high tax pressure. social security schemes can be criticised and social spending can be reduced, but savings in social spending can also be made by giving priority to employment among economic objectives. people on direct benefits and unemployment benefits will then be able to exchange their benefits for a real wage. this is another economic recipe, but it is contrary to the treaty. the budget deficit cannot normally exceed 1% of national product without consideration being given to whether the funds are used for investments which may produce much larger deficits in the future. no election in the member states or vote in the european parliament will change this absurd situation, which runs counter to all experience and is a denial of the democratic principles that unite our countries and which allow voters to express themselves and vote for an increase in employment even if this means inflation sometimes exceeding 2%.
- (da) the commission's recommendation and the report can be assessed on the basis of the fundamental problems facing the system on which the euro was built; these are undoubtedly important and obvious problems. i come from a country where the people have the rare privilege of being consulted whenever important decisions, such as participation in the euro, have to be taken. the danish people are very sceptical and refuse to see their national currency swallowed up by the euro. i have no doubt that this scepticism and opposition is shared by a growing number of people in the other member states who are not consulted. i have time to deal with only two points. the first is extremely important for the whole euro issue. the nobel prize winner, mr franco modigliani, stressed that, in his view, the european central bank had used unemployment as an instrument of economic policy to control inflation. that's a key point. the second point i would like to make is directly related to the theme of this motion for a resolution: the political weakness of the system which established the euro. the political superstructure for managing economic policy is lacking. that is why the french government has proposed that the system on which the euro is based should have a genuine economic government. these political consequences will determine whether the danish people will join this project. the question of whether the euro area should be extended to include denmark, sweden and the united kingdom will be decided in denmark by referendum on 28 september, and i can already tell you that the scepticism i mentioned is still growing.
counsel. - (pt) i regret that the author of question 19 is not here, which deprives me of the opportunity to answer on my own country, but i am pleased to be able to answer question 20 by saying that, as the council resolution of 13 december 1997 recalls, the meetings of the euro 11 group are informal in nature and are intended to enable the member states to coordinate, through debate, the issues relating to their common responsibilities in the field of the single currency. no formal decisions can be taken in this context, but they are taken by the ecofin council in accordance with the procedures provided for in the treaty and as recalled in the above-mentioned council resolution. i think that this is clear and that this dual framework operates in accordance with its own logic, namely: an informal dimension and a formal dimension, the ecofin framework. the first meetings have only a coordinating function for the countries which are part of the economic and monetary union and which participate in the single currency.
- (pt) mr president, this concern will disappear when these countries are part of the council, which will not be the euro 11 council but the euro 15 council. in any case, i would like to say that in the meantime, there is nothing to prevent countries from meeting in a smaller committee within the european union than in the normal community context. we all know from the newspapers and from conversations in the corridors that the countries of the european union, and in particular the nordic countries, systematically coordinate their positions in a framework which precedes the community decision-making process. this coordination does not of course presuppose any transfer of power to this specific framework which serves these debates. it is simply a matter of coordinating joint positions and it is quite natural that the countries which have entered the third phase of economic and monetary union should feel the need to find specific ways of expressing their action and that they should raise these questions collectively in the context of ecofin. however, i do not think that the capacity of the other countries is less than it was initially under ecofin. but it must be said that the minority is the minority and that, therefore, it is the democratic logic that works within ecofin, there is nothing to be done about it. that's the union rule.

counsel. - (pt) mr president, the question of 'euro 11' must be one of the greatest mysteries of the community institutions, because it is systematically recurring. with the third phase of economic and monetary union, informal meetings of the euro 11 council have begun to take place, bringing together the finance ministers of the countries that make up the euro area. these meetings are held monthly and precede the formal meetings of the ecofin council, which brings together the 15 member states. the euro 11 meetings mainly deal with issues related to the specific responsibilities that these 11 states share in matters related to the single currency. when the topics are outside this framework, the meetings are extended to all member states, as planned. whenever decisions need to be taken, the ecofin council shall take them in accordance with the procedures laid down in the treaty. this is the guideline of the luxembourg european council of 12 and 13 december 1997. any other mystery imputed to the operation of the "euro 11" is only a matter of concealing this reality and this information. these are public, since the euro 11 agenda is virtually published in the press and then transposed in the ecofin framework, in the presence of all the other countries that are part of the euro.
- (pt) the honourable member's question is interesting: since euro 11 and the institutionalisation of the third phase of economic and monetary union constitute a form of enhanced cooperation predetermined by the treaty, the question is whether there is an acquis created at euro 11 level and transposed into the union of 15. in fact, there is none, although in reality elements of policy coordination between the euro 11 countries are naturally reflected or should be reflected in their behaviour in the ecofin framework. it is quite normal that countries which are part of the actual policy model and are associated with the implementation of policies which concern, for example in this case, the single currency, can define and coordinate a position themselves, but that its ratification takes place only in the ecofin framework. this is important to understand that in the future, with the existence of differentiated integration models in policy, we will see more of these elements and more of these operating formulas. i agree that this will be possible in the future and we shall see if the feira european council commits the integration of enhanced cooperation within the framework of the intergovernmental conference. so we'll probably be recording different models. what can we do about it? for a country like the honourable member's, the problem can be solved by being part of the euro council. but until the authorities of his country take this decision, the euro council will be composed exclusively of the eleven countries that are currently members.
- (da) if i understand the president-in-office correctly, no negotiation in the context of euro 11 has ever been preceded by formal discussions and decisions in the competent council, namely the ecofin council, no acquis has ever been created, but it is an exchange of views on the problems facing the 11 countries participating in the euro. i think i heard the president-in-office say half-heartedly that there were agendas for these informal meetings and that these agendas should be published. i have never seen such an agenda and i would ask the president-in-office to make it clear whether there are any agendas before meetings. the reason why the swedish and danish members are raising these questions is that we have the impression that it is a very influential body, but if i understood the answer given by the president-in-office correctly, it is still the ecofin council which has the formal powers and can take the final political and legal decision.

- (da) in official speeches, everyone is looking forward to the idea of an enlargement of the eu from 15 to 30 member states, but this enlargement is taking longer and longer and the gathering of european nations is facing a growing number of obstacles. the feira summit will give the green light to particularly close cooperation between the most developed nations and the most federalist ideas. these countries want to create a state within the state and decide for everyone else. such a pattern is known; holding companies are created on the same model, where a small majority manages one company, which in turn manages another. within the eu, we can see a franco-german axis that will set the pace for a centralised eu which, in turn, will decide for the eu as a whole and dictate to the candidate countries the legislation to be adopted while voting laws in the face of all the other european nations. human rights, culture, transport, foreign and security policy could just as well be the responsibility of european bodies such as the council of europe and the organisation for security and cooperation in europe. the single market could also be opened up to all european countries. the countries could also come together in different regional groupings such as the baltic states and the mediterranean countries. some countries should be able to remain outside community cooperation, to be exempted from it, provided that a country does not derive a particular economic advantage from it. some countries should also be able to enter into close cooperation, but it is questionable whether this should necessarily be done within the eu institutions. cooperation can very well take place elsewhere. my group will oppose the principle of majority decisions in the area of enhanced cooperation. we would like more flexibility; we don't need a self-proclaimed vanguard making decisions for us. on another point, i think that the sanctions against austria should be lifted.

in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats in the european parliament today voted neither for nor against the joint resolution on the preparation of the feira european council to be held on 19 and 20 june 2000. we have taken this decision because the declaration sends out some important political signals on the follow-up to the lisbon summit in march, namely signals on employment, the economy and the fight against social exclusion through an open method of coordination, but also on the actions to be taken to avoid unfair tax competition and the measures to be taken to guarantee healthy food. the resolution does, however, contain a large chapter on the current intergovernmental conference and we would like to reiterate in this connection that the fact that we have decided to vote in favour of the entire resolution does not in any way change our basic position that an enlargement of the agenda, a 'constitutionalisation' of the treaty, including the inclusion of the charter of fundamental rights in the treaty with legal effect, will not facilitate a satisfactory result in nice next december, which is essential if we want enlargement to be achieved when the countries are ready. that is why we cannot vote on these chapters.
in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats support the proposal for a european security and defence policy, which will be discussed at the feira summit in portugal in june 2000. the proposal contains ideas which are both clear and sensible in the implementation of a common security and defence policy. the danish social democrats, however, believe that caution should be exercised on either point. the european parliament should be involved in a security and defence policy. we have reservations about the influence of the european parliament in this area, because we believe that security and defence policy must remain primarily a matter between states. the proposal provides for increased use of majority decisions - beyond what is provided for in the amsterdam treaty on the common security and defence policy. we do not see why the scope of majority decisions should be extended to this area. as regards the future investment needed in new technologies and in the acquisition of new military equipment, we believe that these things should be decided on the basis of the priorities defined by the national parliaments in their defence budgets. there is no reason to want to increase european defence budgets. we would also like to remind you of the derogation granted to denmark in respect of all joint actions in the field of defence.
- (da) mr president, a year ago the current secretary-general of nato, mr george robertson, described nato's mission in kosovo in a clear and concise manner. he said: "our aim is to expel the serbs, bring nato back and bring the albanians home". mission accomplished ! it is a victory for human rights and the spirit of justice. i was here before the war started; the serbs had already expelled 200,000 - 300,000 people, they had already devastated, burned and destroyed the entire territory. i went back to the region a few weeks ago and i could see how much things had improved thanks to the intervention of nato. i also saw the excellent work done by kfor, the un mission in the unmik and the unmik police. unfortunately, i have also had to note - and this is where the debate should be focused - that it is now the serbs who are being stoned; they're being bullied, their homes are being burned, and people are trying to evict them. it is the serbs who now require our attention. the police are doing their best, but we still don't have the number of officers we promised. the eu countries have been the most evasive: only one fifth of the police forces are from eu member states. this explains in particular the ease with which the former liberation organization, the kla, can carry out its criminal activities against serbs; the report also mentions murder, arson and attempted evictions. what we need can therefore be summed up as succinctly as it was a year ago: our aim is to expel the kla, to bring the police in and to bring the serbs back to their homes, that is to say, to the localities in kosovo where they have always lived. the kla must be disarmed and its monopoly position removed in the next election campaign; the privileges he apparently obtained in recruiting the new police must also be taken away. the criminal acts committed by the kla must be stopped. eu countries must send more police and judges. we must help the serbs to return home. we must also try to help certain groups of serbs in serbia. on the other hand, it is absolutely essential that the elections scheduled for october in kosovo take place, even if the serbs in kosovo decide to boycott them, because the albanians must first of all be held accountable. at present, all albanians are perceived as "good" and it is the others who are accused of terrorism, street fighting, etc. what we need to do now is to empower the albanian majority.

- (da) mr president, the eu has so many things on its agenda that it is difficult to know when each new episode begins. the primacy of community law dates back to judgments of 1962 and 1964. the right of veto adopted under the luxembourg compromise was in force from 1966 to 1986. the transition to majority voting dates back to the new council of ministers regulation of july 1987 and the provisions in the single act to make majority voting compulsory in the single market. the possibility of enhanced cooperation was introduced in the amsterdam treaty, but unanimity is required, and we have never used this possibility, except in areas where unanimity was known in advance, such as emu and schengen cooperation. at the feira summit, eu member states for the first time recognised that enhanced cooperation could be implemented even if not all member states agreed. the feira summit put this problem on the agenda of the intergovernmental conference. it is too early to say whether it will end with a general clause or a particular clause, but the first split was in view of the abandonment of the principle of equality between countries. germany and france will now be able to set the pace despite the fact that britain and other eurosceptic states want cooperation to take a different path. officially, any amendment to the treaties will still require unanimity, but in practice it will soon be possible to strengthen cooperation without having to wait for the last ones. those who want more integration can congratulate the portuguese presidency, but for those of us who want more democracy and equality between countries, the feira summit is hardly a cause for celebration. i would add that i think the sanctions against austria should be lifted - better today than tomorrow.

- (da) madam president, the framework agreement is a step forward, but it is also a step backwards as regards members' access to information from the commission. my group and the sos democracy intergroup therefore recommend that the vote be postponed so that this agreement can be properly examined in all the parliamentary committees, mainly in the committee on constitutional affairs, the legal affairs committee and the committee on budgetary control. we share the criticism of mrs theato, the chairman of the committee on budgetary control, that the agreement is in direct contradiction with article 276 of the treaty, which guarantees us access to information in the discharge procedure. the commission now has the possibility to determine what information it wants to provide for controls. this is the same procedure as for the ombudsman. the framework agreement is also contrary to our own rules. article 64 explicitly requires, in the context of the transmission of documents by the commission, equality between the council and parliament in the legislative procedure. under the agreement, only the president of parliament and the chairmen of parliamentary committees will be able to examine the various types of non-official documents. the rapporteurs will not be able to demand them, let alone the members. as members of parliament we should have the right to obtain from the commission documents which it gives to others. all the group chairmen agreed on this point when they started discussions with the commission. a compromise has now been worked out so that the people who will be checked can decide what will be checked. such a compromise is not in line with the commitments made by mr prodi. i therefore ask you to reject the proposal today so that it can be improved and we can vote for an improved version next time.
- (da) madam president, my group will vote against the framework agreement. we are convinced that a good examination in the various parliamentary committees and further discussions with mr prodi will yield better results, which we can then approve. let me first correct a misunderstanding. we do not want to prevent the commission from debating in complete confidence. each institution should have an internal phase to discuss all the ideas put forward, to examine all the proposals submitted without all of them having to be published. the commission is also entitled to have confidential drafts throughout the preparatory phase. what we are asking for is access to documents from the moment the commission distributes a document or - and this is not so uncommon - if there are leaks. once a proposal has left the commission, the commission should not be able to prevent us from reading it. everyone must be equal and that is not what the framework agreement provides. we are faced with two humiliating situations that should disappear. as part of the legislative process, you all know that we are considering draft legislation in committee. we have behind us members of the permanent representations, students and trainees who have confidential drafts called 'restrained', which we members cannot obtain. the lobbyists who are also behind us also have these documents, but the elected representatives of the people cannot get them. it happens as often as the press reveals leaks. we are asked for our opinion on the commission's leaked proposal, but we cannot get it. this is a humiliating situation and totally unacceptable. when mr prodi took office, he promised to put an end to this situation. we must have the right to obtain documents from the moment they leave the commission. and we must get that right as members and as rapporteurs, not by kneeling before the president. we could have a president we don't want to bend our knees to.
in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats voted against the framework agreement because it does not, in its present form, comply with the conditions governing the open and transparent transmission of information between the commission and parliament. article 276 of the treaty requires the commission to provide parliament with all the information necessary for the discharge procedure. the framework agreement restricts this provision. under the framework agreement, only the president of parliament, the chairman of the committee on budgetary control and the conference of presidents may request information from the commission. the rapporteur and the ordinary members may therefore be denied access to information. on the political side, it is unacceptable that the chairman of the parliamentary committee should be able to decide what kind of information can be passed on. the legal aspect of this situation is also questionable, since, in accordance with the rules of procedure, the responsibilities are delegated to the rapporteur. in practical terms, such a system will increase the duration of the discharge procedure. the framework agreement requires parliament and the commission to agree on the documents to be provided. it is not normal for the commission (which must be subject to control) to be able to decide, with others, what kind of information to provide. the provisions on confidential documents will also be time-consuming and could be abused by the commission to avoid having to provide information. we hope that it will be possible to negotiate a better agreement on the provision of information in the discharge procedure. the agreement in its present form restricts the right of parliament to have access to the necessary information under the treaty.
in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats in the european parliament today voted in favour of mr lehne's report on the proposal for a council regulation laying down the list of third countries whose nationals are subject to the visa requirement for crossing the external borders and the list of those whose nationals are exempt from this requirement. we note that in its proposal the commission considers that this proposal is not covered by the derogation obtained by denmark despite the fact that in this particular case it is a positive list, which we believe is covered by the derogation. we have decided to vote for the report, however, because we believe that a solution must be found which satisfies all the countries and which respects the derogation obtained by denmark.

- (da) mr president, i think that both the commission and mr arvidsson should be congratulated. i thank the commission because, despite the criticism, it has put forward a proposal in this area and i thank mr arvidsson because i think his report provides a responsible framework for the use of phthalates in toys. phthalates are a class of chemicals that should be of particular concern, not only in relation to toys but also in general. we know they have a cancer risk and we know they have a hormone risk. i therefore support the proposal to ban all phthalates in toys for children. if we do not use the precautionary principle in this case, i can hardly imagine where it could be used. some argue that a ban on the use of phthalates in toys would have a negative impact on the plastics industry and on employment in this sector. we have also heard this argument in the committee on the environment and i think it is totally unacceptable. if we were to accept such an argument, we should prevent any attack - whatever it may be - against the use of hazardous chemicals, not only in toys but also in food and other products. i would have preferred that the ban we are introducing also applied to toys for children aged three to six. that would be ideal, but i am prepared to vote for this compromise proposal, namely the requirement of a warning label against phthalates on toys for this category of children as a first step. i also think that the directive should be re-evaluated in two years' time on the basis of new scientific knowledge, including whether we should also tackle phthalates in other products and not just in toys. that would be, in my view, a smart health policy.

- (da) it is important, mr president, that women can benefit from favourable and safe environments during pregnancy and childbirth so that the mother and child can start on a good footing, and it is also important that pregnancy and maternity do not constitute an obstacle for women on the labour market. we have a directive from 1992 which establishes minimum rights to maternity leave and financial benefits for women when maternity takes them out of the labour market. the commission has just reviewed the application of this directive in the member states and mrs damião's report rightly points out that the commission's report merely makes a technical and factual assessment of the application of the directive without assessing to what extent it actually offers better conditions for pregnant women or women who have just given birth. the rapporteur therefore considers that the commission report does not provide a sufficient basis for revising the directive. i fully agree with the rapporteur on this point. that is why i am also opposed to any current request for revision of the directive. i do not think we should demand a proposal for a new directive, which would give the right to longer compulsory leave and which would contain more detailed requirements for financial compensation without taking due account of the differences in the social systems of the member states. the current rules are very flexible and most countries guarantee women better conditions than the common rules. one may wonder whether new rules would really have a real impact. maternity leave and the granting of financial aid are in all cases a priority within the competence of each state. i think it would be good to have a better picture of the actual situation in the different countries so that governments can exchange experiences - good and bad - and thus inspire each other.
- (da) mr president, adopting a directive does not necessarily mean that the reality is what we want. we have a good example here. the commission's assessment of the safety and health situation of pregnant women seems very good, but is the reality the same as on paper? i'm not so sure. the pace of work has never been higher, efficiency must be maximised and pregnant women must work as if they were not pregnant. this causes stress, which is harmful to the pregnant woman and the fetus. we know this because studies show it. the european parliament as legislator and the members as representatives of the people have a right to know whether the legislation we adopt is also working in practice. this example shows, in my view, the commission's shortcomings in this area. parliament must therefore send a message to the commission that the work done is not enough. it needs to be reworked. we need to know if the legislation is working. the commission's purely legal analyses already show the need for better protection. we need a better definition of adequate benefit and we need stricter rules on dismissal during pregnancy. the commission must get back to work and put forward the necessary proposals. the european social model is characterised by a high level of protection. this must be an area - and this is a remark to the liberal group, whose view is totally different - on which the eu must have a great deal of influence. this area cannot be left to the discretion of individual member states. women's participation in the labour market is a determining factor in the achievement of the ambitious employment targets we have set ourselves and in the modernisation of the european social model. this area must therefore be modernised and action taken. this sector cannot be left to the member states; europe must take care of it together.
in writing. - (da) the members of the uen group voted on 6 july 2000 against the granting of discharge in respect of the implementation of the general budget of the european union for the financial year 1998. we have taken this decision because the present commission, which has committed itself to a wide-ranging reform of its administration, does not show sufficient will to carry out this reform. the many examples of unwillingness, delay and refusal to deliver documents to the committee on budgetary control, and the incredible fact that important reports and minutes disappear from the archives where they should be, are a sad indication of a desire to continue an administration that is synonymous with absolute power. it is clear that the lack of transparency is intended to protect the members and officials of the commission from the sanctions used in any democratic national administration and without which the european union will never function. i have mentioned in the debate cases which have not yet been resolved, but these are merely individual symptoms of a disease. one year after taking office, the commission has still not put forward any proposal to radically amend the provisions relating to the staff regulations; the current statutes offer them legal protection against incompetence and dishonesty, which is totally absurd for an administration that claims to be democratic. the examination of this report by the committee on budgetary control has also shown that some members are more inclined to protect the commission from the european taxpayers than to ensure that the money of those same taxpayers is administered honestly and efficiently. intolerable pressure is being exerted on several rapporteurs and the corrupt circles, which have an interest in preventing the most serious cases of fraud and swindling in the commission's administration from being solved, do not hesitate to resort to physical threats. a majority of members decided today to grant discharge. i do not think that many of them are satisfied with the financial administration of the commission, but we have reached a point where the majority of members would rather turn a blind eye than risk their citizens and voters not being aware of the scale of the scandals that are taking place year after year. i am convinced that the majority of members will regret their complacency in the long run.
in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats in the european parliament voted today in favour of the resolution on the conclusions of the european council held in feira on 19 and 20 june 2000. we have taken this decision because we want to thank the presidency for having implemented, as also reflected in the chapter of the resolution on the follow-up to the lisbon summit, a valid and progressive programme for europe. our overall vote cannot, however, be seen as supporting the wish to extend the programme of the intergovernmental conference and to include the charter of fundamental rights in the treaty with legal effect; we refer you to the explanations of vote we gave at the helsinki summit, the convening of the intergovernmental conference, the position of the european parliament on the intergovernmental conference and the preparations for the feira summit.
in writing. - (da) i am pleased to see that the efforts to liberalize the energy sector seem to be bearing fruit. most countries have gradually started to make major structural changes to the market. some countries have already made full liberalization and i am particularly pleased to be able to say that denmark is in that leading group. as has already happened in sweden in particular, denmark should take advantage of this favourable position to complete this process of liberalisation. i therefore regret the apparent lack of a plan on the part of the danish government to take the final step towards full liberalization. the excellent report by mr mombaur rightly addresses several points. in particular, it is important to take account of the situation of the applicant countries from now on. the liberalization of the energy market in the eastern european countries will require special measures and special attention will have to be paid to the environment. attention should also be drawn to the need to make all electricity production transparent and to the fact that any aid to this sector may in no case lead to a distortion of competition.
in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats voted today in favour of mr karas' report on the communication strategy to be adopted during the introduction of emu. the introduction of the new common banknotes and coins will be of great practical importance to citizens and businesses - not only because the coins and notes will be new, but because they will have to get used to the 'new prices' expressed in the common currency. money is something you have to trust. we must therefore have the information we need - and it must be more complete than the information we have when countries introduce new designs on banknotes and coins today. we stressed in our vote the need to ensure that information campaigns are carried out in the various countries by the member states and that the various countries conclude specific agreements in this regard. it is true that the danish government has not concluded such an agreement, but we hope that denmark will do so after the danish people have voted - we hope - in favour of the common currency in the referendum scheduled for 28 september 2000.

- mr president, in the liberal group we emphasize that this is not a question of creating a single diplomatic corps to replace the foreign services of the member states. the aim is to improve the quality of the community's existing foreign service and to improve cooperation between it and the foreign services of the member states. there is no doubt that such improved training is needed in most member states, and not least in the applicant countries, which will soon need a substantial staff of well-educated people. in the liberal group we emphasize that this is about continuing training and not basic training. in other words, these are training opportunities for people who are already in service or are on their way to service, and we are making sure that the training opportunities are open to participants from the national foreign services, so that there is an interaction between eu diplomats and national diplomats. that is why we have tabled some amendments which we hope parliament will accept. there is a debate about whether a new institution is needed, with a building, with a board and with a big dispute about where the institution should be located. we would prefer to support activities rather than bricks, but we can wholeheartedly support the intention of the proposal and we hope that mr galeote's ideas will be carefully considered in the member states, in the commission and in the council.

in writing. the five members of the left here in parliament support the efforts in this report to improve the training of eu diplomatic staff working in the european commission delegations. we have tabled an amendment which has been accepted, which emphasizes that this is simply retraining and that the courses will be open to diplomats from national diplomatic services. the left, on the other hand, does not support the idea of establishing a new institution in the form of an eu diplomatic school, nor would it like the current diplomatic representations of the member countries to become eu embassies. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full.
in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats in the european parliament voted in favour of the initiative to improve the community's common foreign service and we welcome this initiative. we also support the idea of offering additional training to european civil servants working for an external company. we are, however, opposed to the establishment of a diplomatic school for the training of staff. such training can only be organised, for example, as part of continuing training or field training. we also have reservations about the idea of turning commission delegations into real embassies. this would lead to confusion as to the tasks of the national embassies.
in writing. - (da) like the few democracies in the world, our states have a special responsibility. like the united states, we must be able to take independent initiatives and intervene in those parts of the world where human rights are to be defended and democratic values are to be extended. such interventions must first take the form of financial pressure, but depending on the circumstances, military intervention may be necessary. the eu can only deploy the energy required to carry out such tasks through close collaboration within the union, both economically and financially, and in particular through closer collaboration between external services. at present, the designations of the commission delegations are not homogeneous because of the divergent attitudes within the member states. the eu can only make its voice heard on the political stage if it improves the coordination of its foreign policy, i.e. by forcing the member states to pool their strengths and speak with one voice.
- mr president, our relations with turkey must promote democracy, stability and consistency. the way to do this is through social and economic development, and the way to social and economic development is through foreign investment. the union's financial package will be a driver for further investment in turkey and will thereby also promote and consolidate democracy and human rights. we have seen this strategy work in former dictatorships in southern europe, we will see it work in eastern and central europe, and it should work in turkey as well. this strategy must be supported by parliament, while insisting that the copenhagen criteria - in terms of democracy and human rights - must be met before there can even be any discussion of membership. finally, i would like to stress the need to rebuild the thousands of kurdish villages that have been razed to the ground. it should be an explicit priority in our policy towards turkey and we must insist that the turkish government also make this a high priority, just as we must insist that the rights of the kurdish minority be respected, just as the rights of minorities are respected in the countries of the union and in the other applicant countries.
- mr president, first of all i would like to thank the chairman of the committee on fisheries, mr varela, for the excellent work he has done on these reports. in principle, we in the liberal group support the eu having fisheries agreements with third countries, but i would also like to make it clear that we in the liberal group want - and that is why we have tabled some amendments - that fishermen or shipowners should contribute more to the financing of fisheries agreements. it is quite natural that they should help finance the agreements which benefit them most. fisheries agreements are of particular importance in certain regions where, in addition to employment in the fishing sector itself, there are a number of secondary activities which are wholly or partly dependent on fishing. fisheries agreements allow the eu to continue fishing activities and exchange fishing rights with third countries. the impact of the eu fleets on fisheries resources - in terms of structural policy and in terms of the financial situation - should be analysed much more than is the case. i would like to conclude by asking the commission to provide the committee on fisheries with an account of the positive and negative effects of the eu fisheries agreements on third countries.

in writing. - (da) the danish social democrats in the european parliament welcome the initiative on the establishment of common rules for family reunification, although we voted against certain points such as the one-year rule. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work.
in writing. – (da) the meps of the left regret that, because of the danish reservation, the danish government has not been able to influence the directive and is the only government not to have the right of veto, just as we regret the fact that, unlike other countries, denmark will not make more demands of immigrants to denmark who wish to reunite their families and require them to be self-supporting and to have somewhere to live. the draft directive’s proposal concerning the right to family reunification after one year would not be any problem if, in common with other countries, denmark were to make such demands, as expressly laid down in the draft directive. in that case, it would not be the local authorities’ task to support and find homes for the people concerned, as it is at present. as matters stand, the one-year rule cannot, however, be practised in denmark without significant problems. we therefore decline to vote on the report, regretfully because we should like to see a common policy, especially in the field of asylum.
- mr president, as several speakers have already mentioned, falls from heights are one of the most serious groups of accidents at work we have, and with this report, which is part of an extension of the directive on safety and health in connection with the use of work equipment, we are taking steps to deal with the problem. i would like to say on behalf of the eldr group that, on the whole, we regard the result as progress. the rules we propose to introduce here are generally clear and at the same time seem to respect the need for companies to be able to put the rules into practice and to make them work in everyday life. we must remember that, although the purpose of what we adopt may be good and praiseworthy, the consequences may not always be what we hope for, and therefore it must always be our aim to formulate the simplest and most precise rules possible and to ensure that there is room for the flexibility which is necessary in the labour market. i think that our rapporteur, mr peter skinner, has tried to strike this balance between the desire for clear rules and the need for flexibility and diversity, and i should like to thank him for that and, in general, for his consensus-seeking approach. on the other hand, i think it will be important in the future to work on reducing the risk of accidents by gaining more knowledge of how these accidents happen. how many accidents are there? when do they occur? how do they happen? what can we do to prevent them? i believe that the work that has been started with the european agency for safety and health at work in bilbao will make a positive contribution to reducing the number of accidents by finding the answers to these questions and applying them constructively. this is the right way forward: the authorities in cooperation with the social partners.
